4. Basics of CAM editing                                                                                                                                Using the Project Assistant

Overview: From the model to the NC program                                                                                                                                 Use the Project Assistant to create a milling project or MillTurn project in a predefined sequence of steps.
                                                                                                                                                                           The following are defined in the individual steps:
                  The modular structure of hyperMILL creates a flexible workflow from the model to the NC program.
                   Important elements of this workflow include:                                                                                                                  · Model and machining technology
                                                                                                                                                                                 · NCS orientation
                         1. CAM/CAD system                                                                                                                                       · Stock size and allowances
                         2. Specifying the basic settings                                                                                                                        · NCS position
                                                                                                                                                                                 · Machining frames
                             Define the measurement system and storage directories for hyperMILL data. Configure the dia-                                                        · Names for joblist, milling area/turning area, stock as well as material and machine.
                              logue control, configure the tool and macro database and define further basic settings.                                                      The steps can be in any order. Click OK to apply the settings you have defined. Click Cancel to close the
                         3. Define workpiece origin and frame(s)                                                                                                           Project Assistant without savings the settings.
                              With the origin reference system (NC system), establish a connection to the machine coordinate                            Starting the Project Assistant
                              system.                                                                                                                                      Go to the Jobs tab, right-click and select the New  Project Assistant ... function.
                              Using a frame, define the current machining side and machining direction.
                              Use origins to control the NC output and apply origins when defining transformations.                                                                         Alternatively, use the hyperMILL Project Assistant menu to start the Project Assistant.
                         4. Define tool
                              Define a new tool or apply a tool from the tool database.                                                                 Define model and machining technology
                         5. Prepare manufacturing geometry (contours, surfaces, stock model)                                                                                                 Model and Process: Select Milling technology to create a milling project or MillTurn tech-
                              Both 2D contours and CAD models can be used for 2D machining.                                                                                                  nology to set up a MillTurn project.
                              3D, 5-axis and turning jobs are based on a CAD model.
                              A stock model can be defined for various machining operations.                                                                               Use Surfaces to select the surfaces or solids to be machined.
                         6. Structure CAM project (define job list and jobs)                                                                                               Stock resolution: Defines the accuracy for calculating the tessellated bounding box used to create the
                              Define job and job list.                                                                                                                     stock.
                              Use compound jobs to structure the workflow within a job list.
                         7. Collision check preparations                                                                                                Define NCS orientation
                              Specify a milling area or turning area to define the collision-checked part of the CAD model.                                                         NCS Orientation: Define the orientation of the NCS.
                              Define a collision-checked clamping area to firmly fix the workpiece during machining.
                         8. Calculate and analyse toolpaths                                                                                                                Use one of the options provided for milling: Workplane, Surface or 3 Points.
                             Calculate toolpaths either directly in the job definition or in the hyperMILL browser.                                                        Reverse the direction of the Z axis of the NCS (1) using Z direction  Reverse.
                             Analyse toolpaths via the hyperCAD-S menu hyperMILL  Analysis toolpath. For further informa-                                                  Select an element (line, edge of the model) under X direction  Select to define the direction of the X axis.
                             tion, see the product documentation for hyperCAD-S.                                                                                           Alternatively, use the option 2 Points / Point to align the X axis.
                         9. Simulate machining and generate NC program
                             Simulate machining using the internal simulation, the internal machine simulation, the hyperVIEW                                                                                                          31
                             program, the hyperMILL VIRTUAL Machining Center or an external simulation program.
                             Output an NC program either via the Generate NC file shortcut menu in the hyperMILL browser or
                             using hyperVIEW.

NC system and frames                                                                                                                   All definitions for the vertical machining area are in relation to the frame (top, bottom), clearance distance,
                                                                                                                                       XY plane, horizontal stepover.
                   With the origin reference system (NC system), establish a connection to the machine coordinate system.              The frame shares responsibility for collision checking during machining.
                                                                                                                                       Surfaces that cannot be recognised from the tool axis perspective (1) are not machined.
       Using a frame, define the current machining side and machining direction.

       Use origins to control the NC output and apply origins when defining transformations.

NC system
                   The NC system defines the position of the workpiece origin and the orientation of the machining axes
                   (XYZ).

       The workpiece origin must match the reference point of the workpiece on the machine.

       hyperMILL calculates all the toolpaths in the NC system. The NC system applies to an entire job list at one
       time.

Define NC system

                   When a new (empty) joblist is opened, the NC system aligns with the world workplane. Before a joblist is
                   defined, the NC system must be adapted to the machining conditions. These changes can be made via the
                   joblist (Setup dialogue page) or the frame definition.

Frame                                                                                                                                  In hyperMILL, the first point of a toolpath can also lie below the level given by the height of the machining
                                                                                                                                       side in the z+ direction. This generally means below the uppermost point in the model. The positioning
       A frame defines the current machining side and orientation. In multi-axis indexing, a frame has to be                           movements made by the machine axes up to this first point are not represented in the toolpath, but defined
       defined for each machining side. The assignment of the frame to a job takes place in the job definition.                        in the postprocessor. An OPEN MIND postprocessor positions the rotational axes first, and only then
                                                                                                                                       proceeds to the first point of the toolpath along the linear axes. This needs to be taken into particular
       (1) NC system                                                                                                                   consideration during 5X programming, for example, when linking with the Clearance mode  Radial option
                                                                                                                                       or when linking triaxial toolpaths with differently oriented frames.
       (2-4) Frames

Associative Workplane

                  An associative workplane is linked in hyperCAD-S with the entities, selected for creation. Modifications
                  to the entities are carried over to the linked workplane. To use an associative workplane in hyperMILL,
                   proceed as follows:

                       1. Activate Parametric Modelling in hyperCAD-S,

                       2. select the desired Frame or Origin in hyperMILL,

                         3. activate the Associative workplane function on the dialog page Definition and select the desired
                              workplane.

                                          An associative workplane is marked with a lock symbol in the hyperMILL browser.

General dialogue page

                   Enter the Name , ID (ID number) and, if necessary, a Comment. All entries can also be changed later in
                   the frame list's tree structure.

       The following applies to tools in the 3D Automatic Rest Machining cycle:
       Reference
       The reference tool is used for the calculation of the rest material areas.
       Type: Type of reference tool may be different from type of machining tool. When the Rest material
       reference cycle option is enabled, the previously unmachined rest material area is selected as the area to
       be machined.
       Diameter: A requirement is: Reference tool > machining tool.

                          If you select bullnose end mill as a reference tool type, machining is only possible in
                          entire part mode!

Prepare manufacturing geometry                                                                                                                                     If a stock model in *.stl format is used for a stock model update, it has to be a closed
                                                                                                                                                                   solid.
                   Both 2D contours and CAD models can be used for 2D machining.
                                                                                                                                                                   By default, stock models with a file size of up to 200 MB are displayed in hyperMILL.
                   3D, 5-axis and turning jobs are based on a CAD model.                                                                                           If the stock file is larger, this is indicated in the browser by the following icon: .
                                                                                                                                                                   The size limit can be adjusted to suit individual requirements in the dialogue Setup 
                   A stock model can be defined for various machining operations.                                                                                  hyperCAD-S document  STL file display.

3D and 5-axis machining                                                                                                                                            Alternatively, open the dialogue via the Job definition on the Setup dialogue page or using the New stock
                   3D and 5-axis machining are based on a CAD model. This contains:                                                                                shortcut menu in the lower area of the hyperMILL browser on the Models dialogue page.

The CAD model                                                                                                                                           The Stock model dialogue
                   The basis for the creation of an NC program for 3D machining is the CAD model created in the CAD
                   program and consisting of surfaces and/or solids. It contains all areas to be machined, as well as adjacent                          General
                   areas that are not to be machined in order to optimize the machining procedure and the collision check. 2D
                   or 3D lines that are used as limitation contours or leading curves for profiles during the job definition.                                      Name/Comment: You only need to specify a name if the stock model is to be generated by extrusion or
                                                                                                                                                                   rotation. A descriptive comment can help you identify the stock model later.
Surface gaps
                   Make sure that no gaps arise between adjacent surfaces or between adjacent surfaces and solids during                                Mode
                   model creation. Large surface gaps can cause unwanted nose-diving of the cutter in the course of machin-
                   ing. Gaps with a size below the used cutter radius are not critical.                                                                            In general, the following options are provided for generating stockmodels: Extrusion, Rotation, Surfaces,
                                                                                                                                                                   From job, File, Bounding geometry, From job chain.
Overlapping surfaces
                   Overlapping surfaces can result in unwanted rest material areas. These areas cannot be removed by Auto-                                         Extrusion: Define a (closed) stockmodel based on a contour and a (positive/negative) offset. It can be used
                   matic rest material recognition, as they are not the result of too large cutter diameters but of intersecting                                   for a stock model calculation and for a stock model update.
                   surfaces.

                                                                                                                                                        Extrusion

NC

         The functions Origin ID (NCS) and NC safety are available with a licence for the hy-
         perMILL VIRTUAL Machining Center or the hyperMILL VIRTUAL Machining Optimizer.
         Please contact your OPEN MIND partner.

Origin ID (NCS)
                   Use global definition: Enable to use the default settings of the VIRTUAL Machining postprocessor for
                   creating the NC file.

                   Origin ID (NCS): Activate to select the origin directly from the list for different projects or setups. Time-con-
                  suming reconfiguration in the hyperMILL VIRTUAL Machining Center is no longer necessary.

NC safety / Postprocessor safe positions
                   When the Postprocessor safe positions option is used, fixed positions in the machine coordinate system
                   are used to ensure NC safety.

                   The positions are stored in the postprocessor configuration and are approached, in particular, for safe
                   positioning (by moving linear and rotary axes). When approaching the positions, the NC file switches to
                   machine coordinates (for example M91 for Heidenhain, SUPA for Siemens).

                                                                                                             Security plane: The height of the security plane is calculated to the given clamping position. The value
                                                                                                             should be selected so that all rotary axes of the machine can be moved without a collision.

                                                                                                             security plane can be defined.

          Example  BC kinematics, (1) Clearance plane not optimised, X axis limit exceeded, (2) Clear-                        NC safety / Optimised tunnel logic
                   ance plane optimised, X axis limit maintained, (3) Linking movement.
                                                                                                                                                                    The Optimized tunnel logic function is specially developed for machines with two rotary
          1                  1                                                                                                                                      axes in the table and the possibility of collision-safe positioning of the tool and spindle
                                                                                                                                                                    by retreating into a tunnel with the Z axis. When approaching and retracting for NC jobs,
                   3                                                                                                                                                all axes except Z are positioned in a rapid traverse block, which significantly reduces
                                                                                                                                                                    non-productive times.

Rule for filename generation: As standard, the file name of the NC file is composed of the following                                                                                            85
elements: Jobname_Job ID.

Increment to job ID: This value is added to the job ID.

Package mode: All NC data for the selected joblist is written to a file. The name of the job list is used as
the file prefix.

Start of Counter: Counters that the programme can output for NC file names. The NC file names created in
single mode are numbered consecutively.

Compound job as single job: Only available in single mode. If the function is enabled, all jobs in a
compound job will be written to a single NC file. The name of the compound job is used for the <JOB>
placeholder in the NC file.

Vector fine interpolation: Enabled by default. The definition of a maximum permissible angle change
makes it possible for vector fine interpolation to control machine movements in the case of vector changes.

If you enable the function, hyperMILL will insert intermediate points into the toolpath in order to avoid any
diverging interpolation of the machine from the calculated toolpath.

(1) Vector fine interpolation disabled. (2) Vector fine interpolation enabled with a Max. angle of 5.

                                                                                                                  Compound job

                                                                                                                                     A compound jobs allows job lists to be structured clearly. You can combine any 2D, 3D or 5X jobs into a
                                                                                                                                     compound job. For example, you can combine a machining section (roughing, finishing) into a compound
                                                                                                                                     job.

         100                                                                                                                                                              101

Linking job
                                                                                                                                                  Parameters dialogue page
             A linking job contains other jobs that can be linked with each other in such a way that approach and retract                                            Linking mode
             movements for the entire machining sequence and all linking movements between the individual jobs can
             be optimised and monitored for collisions.                                                                                                              The following linking methods may be used:

Advantages                                                                                                                                                           2D: Linking of 2D, 3D, drill jobs and turning jobs that share the same frame orientation.

             · Shortened machining times.                                                                                                                            5X: Linking of 3D and 5-axis jobs. It is also possible to link the 2D cycles Pocket Milling and Rest
             · Option to perform complex machining tasks.                                                                                                            Machining in 5X mode.
             · Improved product flexibility.
                                                                                                                                                                     Rapids
Requirements
                   To combine multiple jobs into a linking job,                                                                                                      Specify the mode for the fast travel movements (rapids) of linked individual jobs.

                         · for 2D/3D machining jobs, all sub-jobs must use the same tool,                                                                            Skip first and last: Skip the first infeed movement and last retract movement of the individual sub-jobs.
                         · for turning jobs, all sub-jobs must use the same tool, the tool angles and tool orientation (Invert tool)
                                                                                                                                                                     Keep all rapids: Keep all infeed and retract movements of sub-jobs within the linking job.
                            must be the same for all sub-jobs,
                         · for 3D/5X machining jobs, all sub-jobs must use the same tool.                                                                            Conditions

Transformation dialogue page
                   The current transformation is executed by calculating the linking job and all copies are linked to collision-

Job Optimization                                                                                                                                  No optimization: No jobs are optimised. The sequence of all job steps remains the same.
                                                                                                                                                  Optimization with job grouping: Macro jobs are sorted taking into account the tool sequence within macros.
                       Optimisation of manually created jobs as well as macro jobs with the aim of reducing                                       Jobs with different features but the same tool and the same job parameters are combined into one job
                       the number of tool changes and shortening machine running times.                                                           step.
                                                                                                                                                  Optimization without job grouping: Macro jobs are sorted taking into account the tool sequence within
Optimisation of manually created jobs                                                                                                             macros. Identical job steps with the same tool are not combined into one job step. The job steps are sorted
                   To optimise manually created jobs, use theOptimization (only compound jobs) function on the job list's                         in the order of the tool calls in macros.
                   shortcut menu.                                                                                                                 Manually created jobs, which are located between macro jobs, remain at their positions in the job list.

Compound jobs                                                                                                                  Tool Sorting

                   Group manually created jobs of a job list into compound jobs.                                                                  Optimise macro jobs. Manually created jobs are not optimised.. Requirement: The option Optimization 
                                                                                                                                                  Optimization with job grouping or Optimization  Optimization without job grouping is enabled.
                                      Grouping is also available for macro jobs.                                                                  No sorting: No change in the sequence of tool calls.

the tool or the job type remains the same are combined. There is no change in the job sequence.                                                                      same tool may be changed twice.

Feedbacks / No feedbacks: Turn the feedback on/off (when the mouse pointer is in the browser window).
                                                                                                                                     Transformations
                                                           116
                                                                                                                                                        A transformation is a relative movement within a reference coordinate system. The starting point is a start
                                                                                                                                                        reference (start coordinate system) and the end point is a target reference (target coordinate system). The
                                                                                                                                                        movement can comprise a linear and/or rotating movement.

                                                                                                                                                          Z

                                                                                                                                                                     Z

                                                                                                                                                                                Y

                                                                                                                                                                             Y

                                                                                                                                                 Z                                                X

                                                                                                                                                       Y                                       X

                                                                                                                                                    X

                                                                                                                                                 In hyperMILL, transformation refers to the duplication and positioning of toolpaths for the production of
                                                                                                                                                 identical and/or similar components. hyperMILL supports mirroring, linear patterns, circular patterns and
                                                                                                                                                 general patterns.

                                                                                                                                     Advantages

                                                                                                                                                 · Significantly shorter programming times for identical and/or similar geometries

                                                                                                                                                 · Associativity of the transformation when changes are made to the geometry

                                                                                                                                                 · Flexibility as a result of partial deactivation of the direct connection between the base element and
                                                                                                                                                    the transformed element

                                                                                                                                                 · No limitations for 2D, 3D and 5X machining

                                                                                                                                                 Transformations add a transformation rule to the toolpath calculated. hyperMILL displays the resulting
                                                                                                                                                 jobs / job copies in the form of graphical feedback. When mirroring toolpaths using the Mirror mode 
                                                                                                                                                 Mirror path function of the Transformation dialogue page, only the calculated toolpath is mirrored, not the
                                                                                                                                                 model or stock.

                                                                                                                                                 The NC is output as if a new frame was used for the resulting job. As a result, positioning movements can
                                                                                                                                                 sometimes occur on 5-axis machines when using rotation axes.

                                                                                                                                                    It is possible to use NC control functions (e.g., zero point moves, subprograms, etc.).
                                                                                                                                                    However, they require a special adjustment to the postprocessor. Please contact your
                                                                                                                                                    OPEN MIND partner.

                                                                                                                                     Examples

                                                                                                                                                 1. Circular pattern in the plane with 3D postprocessor (no index postprocessor)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      117
                                                                                                                             hyperMILL                                                                                          hyperMILL

             The postprocessor maps the frame system of all copies on the NCS and creates link rapids on the                            Mirror mode
             clearance plane of the jobs.

        2. Circular pattern in the plane with 5-axis postprocessor
             Depending on the settings, the postprocessor outputs a different table/head orientation for each
             position, if required. Additional movements occur between all copies, which correspond to a safe
             positioning logic of the machine.

                                Linking movements created by the postprocessor are not collision-checked
                                against the 3D model. You therefore need to ensure that the clearance plane is
                                above all collision-relevant elements.

                                                                                                                                                     The Mirror mode option is available if the Manufacturing parameters  Copy function is
                                                                                                                                                     enabled for the mirroring.

        3. Using the frame ID for postprocessors                                                                                                     Enable Mirror path to only mirror the toolpath and not the geometry.
             If your postprocessor requires you to define a frame ID or a frame comment, your postprocessor is
             incompatible with toolpaths that were created using the hyperMILL Transformation function.                                              Advantages: Improved performance, easy to use.
             For this reason, please only use toolpaths without transformations for these postprocessors.
                                                                                                                                                     Limitations: Climb milling becomes Conventional milling and vice versa, no collision check is performed,
        4. Structuring the NC output                                                                                                                 not available for compound job or job list, but only for an individual job.
             For recurring toolpaths that were created using transformations, a simplified NC output is not
             generally provided (for example, as a subprogramme or part of the programme that repeats).                                 Linear pattern
             If you wish to have a suitable simplified NC output, contact your OPEN MIND dealer. Please also                                               You can use a linear pattern in a job, compound job or linking job.
             bear in mind that a fee may be required for postprocessor enhancements.
             For toolpaths generated by a transformation, only one safety check with a tolerance of 0.05 mm                             Define
             will be carried out. Collisions with a sufficiently small overlap may not be recognised under certain
             circumstances. Please check the geometric conditions and parameters of the transformation care-
             fully before machining. This constraint does not hold for the mirror.

                                                                                                                                                                           The mirroring of turning jobs is not possible due to a risk of collision. This also applies to
Mirror                                                                                                                                                                     the Turning drilling mode in drilling cycles.

        This special type of transformation mirrors the base geometry. A prerequisite for this is the symmetry of the                                   Climb milling/Conventional milling
        part used. HyperMILL calculates new toolpaths on the basis of the mirrored geometry.                                                            When mirroring curve machinings in the 2D Contour Milling, 3D Free Path Milling and 5X Contouring
                                                                                                                                                        cycles, a distinction is drawn between whether machining takes place to the side of the contour (options:
        122                                                                                                                                             Left/ Right) or on the contour (option: On contour) .
                                                                                                                                                        (1) When machining takes place on the contour, the curve is mirrored, including the start point, to guaran-
                                                                                                                                                        tee geometrically symmetric material removal.
                                                                                                                                                        (2) When machining takes place at the side of the contour, the direction of the contour in the mirroring is
                                                                                                                                                        inverted to also guarantee equal climb to the contour in the mirrored machining.

        How to apply a mirror:

              1. Enable the mirror in the job, compound job or job list (select the Enabled checkbox) and select the
                   required mirror under Selection.

              2. Enable the Copy option to create a mirror of the original (job, compound job or job list). In the
                  hyperMILL browser, a mirrored copy of the original element is created and associatively linked
                   with the original. All parameters are greyed out in the mirrored jobs and cannot be edited directly.
                   As a result of the direct link (associativity) between the original job and the mirrored copy, all
                   changes to the original job are also taken into account in the copy during the recalculation.

        In mirrored machining jobs, you can remove the link between all parameters to the origi-                                                                      Job list comments are transferred to a new, mirrored job list when a mirror is created.
        nal job and adjust them individually! Right-click the respective location in the greyed-out                                                                   This also applies to defined materials. These cannot be changed in the mirrored job list.
        dialogue and select Unlink. The parameter can now be edited directly in the job copy.
        The associativity between both jobs is restored using the Link function.                                                Mirrors and stock models
                                                                                                                                                   Stock models which are to be reflected, must be in *. format. To mirror a job list that uses a stock model:

Collision checking with the hyperMILL Converter
                  A collision check can either be performed by defining milling areas in the hyperMILL browser or using the
                  hyperMILL Converter (polyhedron model). Model areas located on disabled layers are not converted.

                   You have to create at least one 3DF file (polyhedron model) for each CAD model. Areas with a different
                   accuracy and different stock allowances can be entered in a 3DF file.

                                                                                                                                Workspace
       You can, however, change the tool definition (without recalculating the toolpath file) and use the changed
       tool in the simulation ( Tool from job). To do this, click the Tool line and change the required tool
       parameters (for example, tool length). Then choose the Tool from job option for the tool mode.

       If you want to use any tool from the list of all tools already defined for the model in the simulation, select
       the Tool from tool list option. Then select the desired tool from the list under Tool.

       Tool: The tool used in the job is displayed and can be edited.

          Click the icon to open the tool database.

          Replacing the tool in the machine simulation is intended for analysis purposes only. The
          job must then be recalculated in order to generate a valid toolpath file.

Simulation in hyperVIEW                                                                                                              12       3

                         The simulation in hyperVIEW is based on the calculated toolpaths. It is not an NC
                         code-based simulation. The Spindle and Table elements of the machine model can be
                         included in the collision check.

Part allowance                                                                                                                                                                      following areas: Collisions, Gouge, Contacts, Errors and Warnings. The dialogue tabs Collisions,
                   Use part allowance: Activate if a part allowance should be defined for the collision check. The part                                                             Gouge and Contacts list the program lines in which a collision, a component violation or a contact
                   allowance is then defined either using the settings in the job (if the Use job settings option has been                                                          was detected. The dialogue tabs Error and Warnings display status messages that refer to the
                   activated) or it can be entered directly under Allowance. The tool shape is offset in all directions by the                                                      simulation of the VNC or NC program file.
                   entered value. If -0.01 is entered, a ball mill D10 becomes a ball mill D9.98 during the collision check.

                   Use job settings: Enabled by default. The values defined in the job for allowance, allowance XY and

                                      A check is also performed to see whether a negative allowance or the Chamfer height
                                      parameter is defined for theChamfer Milling on 3D Model cycle. As a component viola-
                                      tion is desired here, no collision message is output in these cases.

                   If the Use job settings option has not been activated, the value for the Allowance can be entered directly.
                   The value that is entered is offset on the shank diameter. If -0.01 is entered, the diameter of the shank is
                   reduced by 0.02 (shank radius -0.01).

                   Only tip: The allowance is only applied to the tool tip (to be activated if, for example, only a small area at
                   the bottom of a cavity is to be machined with a negative allowance).

                   Whole tool: The stock allowance is applied to the entire tool (including thickened shank and holder) (to
                   be activated if, for example, a complete steep area is to be machined from top to bottom with a negative
                   allowance).

Stock contact
                   allow thin shank contact: Activate to use a tool where the shank and cutting edge have been defined
                   with the same diameter. The tool shank may touch the stock. No collision message is output. Use with
                   imprecisely defined tool geometry.

                   derive from stock model resolution: Activate to use a tool where the shank and cutting edge have been
                   defined with the same diameter. Situations in which there is a distance of 0 between the stock and shank
                   are not regarded as a collision. The tolerance required for the collision check is automatically derived from
                   the stock tolerance if the function is activated.

                   If the function is not activated, the value for the tolerance can be entered directly. The value that is entered
                   is offset on the shank diameter. If -0.01 is entered, the diameter of the shank is reduced by 0.02 (shank
                   radius -0.01).

                                                                                                                                                        Analysis

                                                                                                                                                                     Start from the hyperMILL Tools  Analysis menu or the Utilities  Analysis shortcut menu of the job or
                                                                                                                                                                  job list. If you start the analysis function from the shortcut menu, using the Test tool position function, both
                                                                                                                                                                  the Frame and the Tool are taken directly from the job.

                                                                                                                                                                               Click the icon to start the Analysis. Further additional functions are available via their corresponding
                                                                                                                                                                           icons. Click the respective icon and hold down the mouse button.

                                                                                                                                                        Analyse one/two surfaces
                                                                                                                                                                               Display the model in transparent mode.

                                                                                                                                                                        Frame: First, select the frame that you want to use for the analysis. In the graphics window, now click
                                                                                                                                                                  the surface(s) that you want to analyse. The hyperMILL plane analysis starts automatically. The dialogue
                                                                                                                                                                  box contains different information, depending on the surface type.

                                                                                                                                                                  You can analyse the following surface types: plane, fillet, torus, cone, free-form surface and hole.

                                                                                                                                                                  Take analysis results

                                                                                                                                                                  You can save the graphical feedback of the dimensions on a freely specifiable layer.

                                                                                                                                                                               Click the icon to define the layer and enter the name to which the feedback should be saved. Then
                                                                                                                                                                           determine the layer colour by clicking the coloured surface.

                                                                                                                                                        Radius analysis/plane analysis
                                                                                                                                                                           You use radius analysis to analyse the radii of all or individual surfaces of a CAD model. You use plane
                                                                                                                                                                           analysis to analyse a CAD model in terms of its planes.

                                                                                                                                                                  Display the model in transparent mode

                                                                                                                                                                      Show planes
                                                                                                                                                                  Radius/surface information

                                                                                                                                                                  Select the surfaces that you want to use for the analysis.

                                                                                                                                                                        Select the frame that you want to use for the analysis.

                                                                                                                                                                  The radius analysis/plane analysis starts automatically. All radii/planes found are combined into groups in
                                                                                                                                                                  a list display.

                                                                                                                                                                  Use Max. / Min. radius to specify the maximum or minimum size of the radii to be recognised. You use
                                                                                                                                                                  Max./Min. angle to specify the maximum or minimum angle of the planes to be recognized. You use the
                                                                                                                                                                  step value to specify the increments for the size of the respective group.

                                                                                                                                                                  If you subsequently change one of these parameters and press RETURN, hyperMILL automatically per-
                                                                                                                                                                  forms a new calculation that takes account of the modified parameters.

                                                                                                                                                                  Filter

                                                                                                                                                                  You use the Show concave/convex/undercut radii options to specify which radii are recognised. In the CAD
                                                                                                                                                                  model, each radius group/plane group is assigned a colour (see List display, column C) and each group
                                                                                                                                                                  can be individually shown or hidden. To hide a radius group, deselect the check box in column V.

                                                                                                                                                                  To show or hide all groups, right-click in the list display and select All on/All off. To show or hide all
                                                                                                                                                                  selected groups, right-click in the list display and select All selected on/All selected off.

                                                                                                                                                                  If you select the relevant line for a radius group or plane group in the list display, all of the radii or planes in
                                                                                                                                                                  this group are coloured red in the CAD model.

hyperMILL for Autodesk® Inventor®

                   The following CAD utilities are available:

Show surfaces with errors
                     hyperMILL runs a plausibility check on the selected surfaces before each milling area recognition. If one

                   or more surfaces with errors are recognised, you can use this function to save the found surfaces with
                   errors to a layer. You can repair the surfaces (for example, reconstruct) or remove them from the milling
                   area if they are not relevant to CAM editing.

Hide surfaces with errors
                       You can use this function to hide all surfaces that could not be converted correctly when generating the

                   polyhedron.

Clear information about surfaces with errors
                       You can use this function to delete information about surfaces that could not be converted correctly

                   when generating the polyhedron.

hyperMILL for SOLIDWORKS

                   The following CAD utilities are available:

Incorrect converted faces
                     hyperMILL runs a plausibility check on the selected surfaces before each milling area recognition. If one

                   or more surfaces with errors are recognised, you can use this function to save the found surfaces with
                   errors to a layer.

                   You can repair the surfaces (for example, reconstruct) or remove them from the milling area if they are not
                   relevant to CAM editing.

                       Zoom all: Zooms all incorrectly converted faces in the current CAD window.

                       Zoom selected: Zooms the incorrectly converted faces that have been selected in a list in the current
                   CAD window.

                       All off: Hides all incorrectly converted faces in the current CAD window.

                       Show all: Shows all incorrectly converted faces in the current CAD window.

                       Show selected: Shows the incorrectly converted faces that have been selected in a list in the current
                   CAD window.

                       Delete all: Deletes all incorrectly converted faces in the current CAD window and deletes them from the
                   list of incorrect faces.

                   All milling parameters are based on the zero point (A) of the machining (here: top of electrode holder).

Connected features
                   Features linked to the job are listed in the Feature Job Connector. The number of features is displayed.

                                                Click on the icon to open the Select feature dialogue in order to link a feature with
                                                the current job.

                                                Features which should be used in the jobs can be moved to the right window by
                                                double-clicking or clicking on the button (1).

                                                Features which should not be used in the jobs can be moved to the left window by
                                                double-clicking or clicking on the button (2).

                   Feature derived: Production-related feature parameters are represented in the bottom area of the Feature
                   Job Connector. A feature parameter, whose status is set to active, is used in the job definition for
                   calculation purposes. If the status is inactive, the parameter is not used.

                   Formula: Various geometric parameters of the features: Simple Hole, Sink Hole, Free defined hole, pocket
                   (for use in contour milling cycle) and planes can be defined using a formula. All basic calculating opera-
                   tions are permitted as arithmetical expressions for the purposes of defining the formula. In this case, the
                   feature parameter can be used as a variable.

                   View feature: The feature linked in the job is highlighted in the graphics window.

                Generic Pocket
                                   Generic Pockets can be used universally and are structured according to manufacturing elements and
                                   related properties.

                      Generic Pockets offer significant advantages when using macro machining. In particu-
                      lar, when setting up a macro database with macro rules, OPEN MIND recommends
                      exclusive use of generic holes.

                   Geometric data can be used for 2D, 3D and 5X and drilling cycles in the job definition. Geometric
                   parameters are automatically copied for use in the job definition.

Generic turning feature / Turning groove
                   Generic features for turning operations can be used universally and are structured by means of manufac-
                   turing elements and their properties.
                   Geometric data of the Generic turning feature can be used within the job definition for the Rough Turning,
                   Contour parallel Turning and Finish Turning cycles.

               2                                                                                                                             adjust the Tolerance for the grouping.

                                                                                                                                             Import hole / Export hole: Import or export all parameters defined with a hole in *.hmf format. This applies
                                                                                                                                             to the Simple Hole, Sink Hole or Free defined Hole features.

                                                                                                                                             Reference frame: A reference frame can be selected when exporting holes. By doing so, all holes will be
                                                                                                                                             exported with their relative frame coordinates. If this option is not activated, the WCS coordinates are used
                                                                                                                                             when exporting the holes. If the model position has changed, the holes will be correctly imported, if the
                                                                                                                                             same Reference frame is selected.

                                                                                                                                             Use feature lists: When importing the holes, you can enable the Use feature list option to preserve the
                                                                                                                                             allocation of the Features to the Feature lists.

                                                                                                                                   Turbomachinery feature

                                                                                                                                                      Turbomachinery features, after being assigned just once, allow the geometry elements of turbine blades
                                                                                                                                                      and impellers and blisks to be easily and flexibly reused with different machining strategies. Filter defini-
                                                                                                                                                      tions simplify automated machining and macro programming.

                   The following parameters can be defined:                                                                                  Under General, specify the required Name for the feature.

                   Orientation (1): Enter coordinate values or determine the orientation vector from the CAD system.                                    In the Feature parameters area, select the geometric elements.
                                                                                                                                             (1) Blade surfaces, (2) Bottom plane, (3) Hub curve, (4) Shroud surface
                   Contour (2): Specify contour in the CAD system.

                   Wall angle (3): Enter angle of pocket wall or derive it from the CAD system.

                   The pocket height is defined using the properties of the selected contours.

                   For individual contour areas, it is possible to define different Z values (Top / Depth).

Compound Feature
                   Geometric data from the Compound Feature can be used for 2D, 3D, 5X and drilling cycles in the job
                   definition. The following parameters can be defined

                   Frame: Yes if uniform frame required. If this option is selected, only sub features with the same frame
                   system within the Compound Feature are used. No, if uniform frame is not required.

                   Frame origin: Enter coordinate values or determine the orientation vector from CAD system.

                   Frame orientation: Enter coordinate values or determine the orientation vector from CAD system.

                   Sub features: Select previously defined features. Existing features can be added to a Compound Feature
                   directly in the feature browser. To do so, select the feature and use the Compound Feature function in the
                   shortcut menu.

                   Sub features of a Compound Feature can be selected directly in the feature browser and freely moved or
                   reordered (drag-and-drop operations). A Compound Feature consists of referenced sub features. Changes
                   to one or more sub features are automatically passed to the Compound Feature.

Functions on the shortcut menu
                  Point optimisation: hyperMILL optimises the paths to be travelled between the holes of the Compound
                   Feature. To do so, select the feature and then select the Point optimisation function from the shortcut
                   menu. The machining sequence is indicated by arrows. The first step is indicated by a blue arrow.

                   When one of the hole features within the Compound Feature already consists of several holes, these need
                   to be broken up first for the points to be optimised efficiently. To do so, select the feature and then select
                   the Explode function from the shortcut menu. The optimise the points as described above.

                   Reverse sequence: Use this function to reverse the machining sequence for the holes in a Compound
                   Feature.

                                                                                                                                          defined tip angle is larger than the angle in the hole, these holes are recognised as the through hole type.
                                                                                                                                          If the tip angle is smaller, the holes are recognised as the tip hole type.

                                                                                                                                          Example:

                                                                                                                                          (1) Min. tip angle = 75°. Holes 50°/ 60° = Through, hole 90° = Tip.

                                                                                                                                          (2) Min. tip angle = 55°. Holes 50° = Through, holes 60°/90 = Tip.

                                                                                                                                          (3) Min. tip angle = 45°. Holes 50°/ 60° / 90° = Tip

When importing feature information from a CAD model, hyperMILL also recognises feature properties such                               50°  60°  90°  50°  60°  90°  50°  60°  90°
as thread, ISO fits or feature classes if these are defined in a colour table.

hyperMILL evaluates the assignment of colour information to feature properties when generating features
and incorporates the corresponding parameter directly in the feature. At the same time, this parameter is
also included in the feature name and is thus available for selection when creating and using macros.

To read information from a colour table, enable the Use colour table option in the Feature Mapping
dialogue, select a file in *.xml format ( Select colour table) and confirm your entries. In order for hyperMILL
to be able to correctly recognise the Thread and Fits feature properties, they must be defined as follows:

        For each pocket recognised, the functions Close corners and gaps and Close slots are also available in
        the shortcut menu.

        Close corners and gaps (A): Boundary contours of pockets (1) whose holes interrupt the contour (2) are
        closed.

        Close slots (B): Boundary contours of slot-like pockets that are the same length and run parallel to each
        other (1) are closed in a semicircle (2).

        The Slot, Slot profile and Slot width feature parameters are displayed in the feature editor. Proceed as
        follows to machine the recognised slot:

              1. Create a new job (2D Contour Milling) using the corresponding Pocket feature.

              2. Define the tool diameter according to the slot width and specify the On contour option as the tool
                   position.

              3. Select the Active only option from the shortcut menu of the slot profile on the Feature dialogue
                   page.

              4. Define all other parameters.

Pocket type: Through pocket

Pocket

        Complete model: If you select this option, hyperMILL searches the entire model for possible through
        pockets, while taking account of the Frame selected. Click Start to start the search for pockets.

        User defined: Manually select the pocket walls for Pocket Recognition.

        Pocket walls: Select the pocket wall surfaces.

                             248                                                                                        249
                    hyperMILL

        A       BB

Result

        The number of recognised pockets is displayed. Recognised pockets are shown in a list display. In
        addition, the parameters Open/Closed, Height, Edge type (Chamfer/Fillet), Radius/Height, Chamfer angle
        of the respective pocket will be displayed.

Search dialogue page
                   Search for comparison pockets based on the defined sample pocket(s). Limit the search for comparison
                   pockets to a part of the model (option: User) or search the entire model (option: Complete model).
                   Recognised pockets are presented in a list.

Setup dialogue page
                   Limit the search using filter criteria. Define comparison parameters for the search. Define conditions for
                   accessibility of pockets and the creation of feature lists.

Pocket filters
                   Tool diameter: Limit the pocket size using the tool diameter. Only pockets matching the following criteria
                   will be recognised: the upper diameter should not fit in the pocket, while the lower diameter should still fit.

        Circular pocket limit: This option can be used to prevent holes from being recognized as circular pockets
        by defining a diameter limitation. Only pockets that fall within the defined limitation are recognised.

        Chamfer height / Fillet radius: Height of the chamfer or radius of the fillet between the bottom surface and
        the pocket wall.

        Chamfer angle: Lower / upper limit for the chamfer angle between the bottom surface and the pocket wall.

        Allowed wall types: Specify which wall type is to be recognised. The Perpendicular option corresponds to
        the Z axis of the frame that is defined on the Pocket example dialogue page.

        Combine pockets: If the Suppress original pockets option is enabled, the original pockets will be deleted
        after combining.

Pocket comparison parameter
                   These two parameters relate to the comparison between the sample pocket and the pockets that are to be
                   recognised (see Comparison dialogue page).

        Scale limit: The minimum / maximum scale limit defines the recognition of pockets in the model on the
        basis of characteristic geometric parameters.

        Comparison tolerance: The degree of accuracy with which the characteristic geometric parameters (see
        Scale limit) of one or more sample pocket(s) are compared with the pockets to be recognised.
